#2838. Thunder’s Chet Holmgren Makes Controversial Championship Remark Amid Injury Recovery

The Oklahoma City Thunder discovered a small patellar tendon tear in the right knee of center Chet Holmgren, ESPN's Shams Charania reports. Getty

The Oklahoma City Thunder have championship expectations, but keeping their best players healthy has become another challenge. Star center Chet Holmgren is currently recovering from a knee injury, although his latest comments suggest he isn't particularly concerned about the setback.

Holmgren recently discussed his recovery and offered an interesting perspective on missing significant time. Rather than focusing on everything he cannot do, the Thunder big man pointed to what happened the last time he was sidelined for an extended stretch.

"I can still lift," Holmgren said, according to Clemente Almanza. "Sh*t, last time I missed 8-10 weeks, we won a Championship."

It's a confident remark that could raise some eyebrows, especially considering how difficult winning another NBA title will be. However, Holmgren appeared to be making light of an unfortunate situation rather than suggesting Oklahoma City is better without him.

Holmgren's injury has forced him to spend another stretch watching his teammates compete without him. For a player expected to play an important role in Oklahoma City's championship pursuit, that cannot be particularly enjoyable.

However, his latest comments suggest he understands that recovering properly matters more than rushing back onto the court.

GettyThe Oklahoma City Thunder star center Chet Holmgren during a game.

Holmgren has explained that he can continue lifting weights and maintaining strength during rehabilitation, even though explosive basketball movements remain restricted. That distinction is important because it gives him opportunities to stay physically active while his knee heals.

The Thunder have already experienced what life without Holmgren looks like. During their 2024-25 championship season, Oklahoma City overcame his extended absence before eventually capturing the NBA title.

Still, winning a championship despite losing an important player doesn't mean the Thunder should welcome another injury. Holmgren's defensive presence, rebounding and offensive versatility remain important to Oklahoma City's long-term success.

There is something interesting about Holmgren's willingness to joke about another lengthy absence. Most players would understandably express frustration, particularly after already dealing with significant injuries earlier in their careers.

Instead, Holmgren appears focused on what he can control.

His championship reference also reflects the confidence surrounding Oklahoma City's organization. The Thunder believe they have enough talent and depth to remain competitive while important contributors recover.

That doesn't guarantee another championship, and Holmgren certainly wasn't promising one.

However, his comments offer an encouraging glimpse into his mindset during a difficult stretch.

For Thunder fans, the priority remains getting Holmgren healthy before the games that matter most. If Oklahoma City can accomplish that, his championship joke might become an interesting footnote to another memorable season.

"It's been reflective ever since he's been on this team," Wembanayama said of Castle. "He's been impactful in a meaningful way and he's impacted winning ever since he arrived here."

If he can continue to deliver at that level consistently, the bench unit will be much improved as the season goes on. "When I do the right things on the court, the scoring will come," Keldon said after the game.

De'Aaron Fox slipped on a wet spot on the floor during the first half and was limping a bit before being taken out of the game. He would return later and Mitch Johnson said after the game that Fox was fine. Still, Fox scored just 5 points on 1-for-4 shooting and one has to wonder if his hip injury is bothering him.

#2835. Social Security benefits are rising in 2027, but other changes could affect what you keep

Whether you're working or retired, changes to Social Security in 2027 could affect your finances.

Starting in January, millions of program beneficiaries will get a 3.6% increase to their benefits, thanks to the annual cost-of-living adjustment. But rising Medicare premiums could chip away at the increase — and as beneficiaries' incomes rise, that may affect the taxes they owe and their eligibility for a new senior deduction.

Meanwhile, a higher taxable earnings cap means some workers will pay Social Security taxes on more income, reducing their paycheck.

Here's a breakdown of what to expect in the new year.

Social Security's limit for payroll taxes is adjusted every year to reflect the average wage index.

In 2027, the taxable maximum will go up to $192,300 in earnings, up from $184,500 in 2026. Workers and employers each pay a 6.2% payroll tax on earnings up to that limit — or 12.4% for self-employed workers. Medicare taxes of 1.45% for both workers and employers are not subject to a limit.

In 2027, workers who earn $192,300 or more will pay $11,922.60 in total Social Security payroll taxes, according to the Social Security Administration, while self-employed workers will pay $23,845.20.

"If you're somebody whose income is over what the new threshold is going to be, this increase in the threshold results in a tax increase to you," said Tim Steffen, director of advanced planning at financial services firm Baird.

The 3.6% Social Security cost-of-living adjustment for 2027 is slated to increase retirement benefits by about $75 per month on average, according to the Social Security Administration.

The annual adjustment is the largest boost to benefits since 2023 due to rising inflation. The COLA averaged 2.6% from 2000 to 2026, according to the Senior Citizens League, a nonpartisan senior group.

Around 72 million Social Security beneficiaries will see benefit increases, according to the agency.

The maximum benefit for a worker at full retirement age — when individuals become eligible for 100% of the benefits they've earned — will rise to $4,188 per month in 2027, up from $4,152 per month in 2026, according to the federal agency. To be eligible for the maximum benefit at full retirement age — typically age 66 to 67 — you generally have to earn up to the taxable threshold for each of your working years factored into the calculation.

How much of the COLA increase Social Security beneficiaries see depends in part on their Medicare premiums, which may be deducted directly from benefit checks.

Monthly rates are poised to increase for both Medicare Part B, which covers medically necessary services and preventive care, and Part D, which provides prescription drug coverage.

The standard monthly Medicare Part B premium will be $209.40 in 2027 — a $6.50 increase from $202.90 in 2026, according to the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services.

"It's not often that we see a Part B [premium] increase that is lower than the COLA," Mary Johnson, an independent Social Security and Medicare policy analyst, said of the 3.2% increase. The last time that happened was in 2023, she said.

Medicare Part D beneficiaries may also pay higher premiums in 2027.

Assuming enrollees keep the same plan, the average enrollment-weighted monthly Part D premium may be $51 in 2027, up 50% from $34 in 2026, according to projections from KFF, a provider of health policy research and polling. That is higher than CMS' projections, which forecast the national average premium for standalone Part D plans will rise about $1, to $36, in 2027 from $35 in 2026.

Standard monthly premiums for Part B and plan-specific premiums for Part D apply to individuals who have $112,000 or less in modified adjusted gross income, or $224,000 for married couples. Beneficiaries with higher income pay larger premiums because of income-related monthly adjustment amounts, which affect 9% of Medicare Part B and Part D beneficiaries, according to CMS.

With bigger Social Security benefits, more households may find that some portion of benefits is taxable — though a new senior deduction may help provide some relief to those who are eligible.

Social Security benefits may be subject to federal income taxes for certain individuals. The thresholds at which those taxes are applied are not adjusted from year to year.

Whether or not benefits are taxed is determined by a formula known as combined income — the sum of adjusted gross income, tax-exempt interest income and half of Social Security benefits:

Individuals who are subject to taxes may opt to withhold taxes from their Social Security benefit checks, rather than face a potential bill during tax filing season.

Separately, a temporary senior deduction of up to $6,000 for individuals ages 65 and over may help to indirectly reduce taxes on Social Security benefits.

That deduction, part of the One Big Beautiful Bill Act in 2025, helps reduce retirees' taxable income, according to Steffen. It's in place through 2028. Because the break phases out for individuals with modified adjusted gross income starting at $75,000 — and for married couples with $150,000 — it's important to watch your income if you're close to those limits, Steffen said.

"Keep in mind that if you're over 65, you might qualify for this deduction," Steffen said. "But if you let your income get too big, you might lose the deduction."

Moreover, any extra income that causes you to lose the deduction may also come with its own taxes, making it more expensive, according to Steffen.

"What we've been telling our clients is that tax planning is a lot more complicated than it used to be, and you can't do it on the back of an envelope anymore," he said. "You need to use good tools that can do good calculations for you."

Individuals who claim Social Security retirement benefits before their full retirement age and who continue to work may be subject to withholdings.

In 2027, individuals under full retirement age may earn up to $25,560 per year, up from $24,480 in 2026, without adjustments to their Social Security benefits. For every $2 in earnings above that limit, $1 in Social Security benefits will be withheld.

Importantly, those withholdings are not lost completely. Once a beneficiary reaches full retirement age, the withheld money will be credited to their benefits over their remaining lifespan.

In the year individuals reach full retirement age, the retirement earnings test limit is higher — $67,920 per year in 2027, up from $65,160 per year in 2026. For every $3 in earnings above that limit, $1 in Social Security benefits is withheld.

Starting the month a beneficiary reaches full retirement age, no earnings are withheld.

#2834. Where is Red Wings Fire?

The Detroit Red Wings have won just two games in the month of November and find themselves circling the bottom of the Atlantic with only the dysfunctional Toronto Maple Leafs below them in the division standings for the moment. It's not that the Red Wings haven't been in games, however, for a team with very little room for error, it continues to find them, despite their best efforts.

"This is who we have right now, and this is what we're playing with. And we're going to keep working hard and give it our best effort each night," Head coach Todd McLellan said, "I can't hold their feet to the fire for anything other than a good effort."

Detroit has had a tough run in the last month, with every team they've faced ahead of their matchup with the Vancouver Canucks on Nov. 25 at a .500 record or better so far this year. Despite the Red Wings improved defense on paper, the reality has been that in all but one their matchups since Halloween, they've allowed in two or more goals to opposing offenses.

Meanwhile, the Red Wings have only scored more than two goals three times since their first loss to the St. Louis Blues. McLellan described a "gap" between the Red Wings and the elite teams of the league after their loss to the Boston Bruins.

"You know, the same problems arise. We were short on offense, and I thought today we had some good luck early because they hit the post and that helped us. But we had some bad luck on the first one, the way it ricocheted around, ended up in our net," McLellan said,

"And the second one was flat out too many men on the ice, so the officials missed that. But, you know, sometimes when it's not going well, those things come back and go against you rather than for you, so, that's where we're at."

It's a far cry from McLellan's message when he first came to Detroit, firing up a Red Wings squad that was floundering and playing far too mechanically under former head coach Derek Lalonde. Now, for a different set of reasons, the Red Wings find themselves struggling as they head into December and it seems fair to wonder what exactly can be said or done to inspire this Red Wings squad.

The biggest issue of course, is that this team doesn't seem to have enough stars to keep a rotation going. McLellan likes to compare Red Wings stud defenseman Moritz Seider to a diesel engine.

Well, anyone with a diesel in their truck will tell you; You need two batteries to get that thing going.

Diesel is a relatively stable fuel, so it's hard to ignite and get things running like you want it to, especially out in the cold. The thing about it is that it needs a little pre-loaded pressure and heat to actually turn the engine over.

Right now, it doesn't seem like the Red Wings can find the energy to turn over a new leaf and start winning games. For that, they'll need to find raise the pressure they put on not just their opponents, but themselves to win in the gritty, hard areas of the game they haven't so far.

#2833. Broncos vs. Raiders first quarter recap

The Las Vegas Raiders won the opening coin toss and deferred to bring out Bo Nix and the Denver Broncos offense to start this game.

An opening penalty and back-to-back passes to Jonah Coleman for minimal gain setup a third and 11. They hadn't converted any third downs and over 10 yards on the season. 0/28. That streak ended on the next play with a Nix strike to Pat Bryant for a 21 yard gain and a first down.

They'd face another third down, this time seven. Nix fired a perfect pass deep over the middle to Jaylen Waddle who dropped the pass to bring out the punt unit.

Fernando Mendoza took his first snap on the road in the NFL on the next drive. He quickly found himself in a third and seven where Alex Singleton exploded off through the middle to sack him, but the Raiders called a timeout just before the snap. On the real snap, Mendoza completed a pass outside to Ashton Jeanty who was tackled just shy of the first down marker to force the three-and-out.

The Broncos' blocked the punt with Sai'vion Jones coming up through the middle to block it.

Denver's offense wouldn't do much from there with a shot to Waddle on third down going through his hands again. Wil Lutz would put the Broncos on the board, though.

After an initial first down on the first play of their next drive, the Raiders found themselves in big trouble after a holding call to setup a 2nd and 20. Jonathon Cooper exploded off the edge to sack Mendoza to setup a third and 28. They would be punting on the next play.

Jonah Coleman began to heat up on the next drive with several good runs, then mixed in a Nix completion to Waddle for 18 yards to get into Raiders' territory. The biggest play on the drive came on a third and nine where Nix found Evan Engram who was able to dodge some tacklers to get the first down into field goal range.

Coleman then burst through a wide open hole in the middle for a nine yard gain to get inside the 10 yard line. Adam Prentice then dove forward for a first down to setup a first and goal at the six yard line.

#2832. Karl-Anthony Towns draws praise from another former coach

Karl-Anthony Towns keeps drawing praise from his former coaches.

Yet another one pointed out how Towns — by playing a central role in the Knicks' championship — disproved much of the criticism that surrounded him throughout his career.

"When we first got him in Minnesota, the playoffs, they were saying how he's a very good player that couldn't win, couldn't get the team to the playoffs," Trail Blazers coach Micah Nori, who served as an assistant with the Timberwolves when Towns was there, said before the Knicks' 97-96 buzzer-beating win Saturday night at Madison Square Garden. "Then, when we got to the playoffs, any time we struggled, it was him that took the fall, unfairly. And I think it was great to see not only the impact that he had on this championship team, but to see the way that he's playing now. … I love KAT; I was happy for him to get to the mountaintop; I was happy for him to play a major role."

Earlier this week, Timberwolves coach Chris Finch shared while the Knicks were in Minnesota a similar sentiment about Towns, who missed Saturday's game due to an illness, shedding a narrative that he isn't a winner.

Karl-Anthony Towns (illness) did not play in the Knicks' 97-96 buzzer-beating win over the Trail Blazers on Nov. 21, 2026, at Madison Square Garden. NBAE via Getty Images

"Particularly when he started his playoff journey, how much flak he had gotten," Finch said.

"It was fun to see him at the heart of it too, especially as they were kind of tweaking their offense going along and all of his skills came to the surface through that."

Jordan Clarkson, who missed Saturday's game due to right plantar fasciitis, was wearing a boot on his right foot while sitting on the Knicks bench.

OG Anunoby was out Saturday due to left calf soreness.

Towns' absence meant a significant role for James Wiseman, who logged 24 minutes off the bench and recorded five points, six rebounds and three blocks.

"I thought James was great," head coach Mike Brown said. "He ran the floor in both directions, he talked defensively, he impacted the ball at the rim in a phenomenal way. I thought he was really good in his minutes."

He went scoreless Saturday and missed all seven shots he took, including four 3s.

#2829. Eagles QB Joe Milton III stood and watched his fumble in shades of infamous Cam Newton Super Bowl play. Then he delivered late to beat the Steelers

Philadelphia quarterback Joe Milton III made an early play to draw the ire of Eagles fans Sunday.

He finished with a big throw to help secure a 19-16 win over the Pittsburgh Steelers and snap a seven-game losing streak.

Milton made his second start for the Eagles Sunday in place of the benched Tanner McKee and sidelined starter Jalen Hurts. He did not do his best to make a good early impression on the home fans.

On Philadelphia's second play from scrimmage, Milton was stripped of the ball in the backfield by Steelers pass rusher Alex Highsmith. But it wasn't the fumble that's likely to draw the ire of Eagles fans. It was his response to the loose ball.

Milton wasn't tackled on the play. And as the ball hit the ground near his feet, he just stood there and looked at it.

Milton made no effort to secure the loose ball that he had a chance at recovering.

Fortunately for the Eagles, left tackle Jordan Mailata did make a play on the ball and was able to secure it before Highsmith could. It was a good break for the Eagles that could go a long way in saving Milton from heat in Philadelphia.

The Eagles were able to convert the recovery into an early 3-0 lead.

The early field goal helped the Eagles to a 13-0 first-quarter lead. Philadelphia's defense did the work from there, holding the Steelers out of the end zone until the final two minutes of the game.

But the game wasn't quite over after the late Steelers score. Philadelphia got the ball back with 1:21 remaining with a 3-point lead in need of a first down to secure the win. Milton delivered with a 7-yard completion to Devonta Smith on third-and-6 to ice the win.

Milton finished the game completing 11-of-21 passes for 141 yards without a touchdown or an interception.

The first-quarter play drew immediate visions of Cam Newton. The former Carolina Panthers great made — or didn't make — a famously familiar play with considerably higher stakes attached.

With his Panthers trailing the Denver Broncos by six points in the fourth quarter of Super Bowl 50, Newton was stripped of the ball by Broncos pass rusher Von Miller.

Like Milton on Sunday, Newton had a shot at recovering the ball and made no effort to do so. But Newton's Panthers weren't as fortunate as the Eagles on Sunday.

The Broncos recovered Newton's fumble deep in Carolina territory to help seal a 24-10 Denver win.

Milton's lack of effort clearly won't go down in infamy like Newton's. And he found himself in the good graces of Eagles fans by game's end.

#2828. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

Berkshire's top holdings of disclosed publicly traded stocks in the U.S. and Japan, by market value, based on the latest closing prices.

Holdings are as of June 30, 2026, as reported in Berkshire Hathaway's 13F filing on August 14, 2026, except for:

#2827. 3 thoughts ahead of the Mavericks’ back-to-back bonanza at the Utah Jazz

It may feel like the season just started, but the Dallas Mavericks (9-7) are already in the middle of the team's fourth back-to-back set as they turn the team charter toward the great Salt Lake, with a quick turnaround before facing the Utah Jazz (3-14) at the Delta Center. The Mavericks beat the Los Angeles Clippers (4-11) at home on Saturday, 110-100.

Dallas salvaged a clunker on Saturday, and if there is one thing the Jazz are accustomed to this year, it's clunkers.

Let's take a swim in the salty waters out west and get a look at what lies ahead for the Mavs in Utah.

The back end of a back-to-back set for the Mavericks is the front end of one for Utah in this one. The Jazz are coming off of Friday's 111-101 home loss to the Phoenix Suns. Rookie guard Darryn Peterson was gaining some steam earlier in November with five straight games of 21 or more points before a three-game lull preceded his 20-point night in the loss to the Suns. Still, he's shooting just 36.3% from the field and just 26.1% from 3-point range in his last four.

While Peterson is averaging 16.7 points per game through the first 17 games of his NBA career, Keyonte George leads the team at 21.1 points and 7.1 assists per game, while key contributors Lauri Markkanen (hand), Jaren Jackson Jr. (knee), and noted Mavs killer Jusuf Nurkic (foot) will all miss Sunday's game with injuries. As a result, the Jazz come into the game against Dallas with an offensive rating of just 106.9, next to last in the league, ahead of only the Charlotte Hornets (103.1). They're 25th in the NBA in net rating, at -7.7.

The Mavs had some trouble putting the struggling Clippers away in the front end of their back-to-back set on Saturday, but Sunday's game in Utah presents another opportunity to hone that edge to their game that has been hit-and-miss since the team's 7-4 start to the year.

Keep an eye on who is available for the Mavericks ahead of Sunday's game in Utah. Kyrie Irving, who scored 25 points on 8-of-24 shooting in 35 minutes in Saturday's win over the Clippers, has played in both legs of one of the three previous back-to-back sets so far this season. We're just guessing, but the established trend and the opponent offer the Mavs another opportunity to do the "injury management" dance.

The Mavs were also without PJ Washington (illness) on Saturday, and while the hope would be that one game off would be enough to get Washington right, we won't know for sure until about noon on Sunday for sure on their status. If those two are unable to go, the viewing experience could end up anywhere from "meh" to downright brutal.

Lost in the slog of Saturday's squeaker over the Clippers was a rare good shooting night from Max Christie. On a night when Cooper Flagg's shot diet (6-of-16 from the field) was downright unhealthy and the rest of the offense sputtered around him, the Mavericks needed all 13 of Christie's points in his 24 minutes on the floor after he had worked his way into the Dusty May Phantom Zone after a slow start to the year.

Both Christie and Walter Clayton Jr. (16 points, 5-of-8 shooting, 2-of-3 from 3-point range) were positives off the bench for the Mavericks, and they will both likely be called upon to do it again on Sunday, especially if Irving is inactive.

The Mavericks and the Jazz tip off at 6 p.m. on Sunday at the Delta Center. The game will be televised locally on KFAA Channel 29, or you can catch the stream on MavsTV or NBA League Pass where available. The radio call can be heard on KRLD 105.3 The Fan.

#2826. ‘More than just a game’: How GTA VI’s blockbuster launch fuels a booming content economy

Grand Theft Auto VI has exploded out of the gate after a 13-year wait, drawing millions of buyers while fueling a parallel economy of livestreams, clips and other content built around people watching the game as much as playing it.

GTA VI has delivered a blockbuster debut, with about 19.1 million copies sold — including preorders — by Thursday morning, representing roughly $1.84 billion in sales, according to Sensor Tower. The GTA franchise itself has sold more than 470 million units worldwide.

Take-Two Interactive, which owns the studio behind the game, has not yet disclosed a profit figure for the title.

Ross Gerber, president and CEO of Gerber Kawasaki Wealth & Investment Management, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" that he sees further upside for Take-Two, which his firm holds in its portfolio.

"It's crazy good. I've never seen anything like it," Gerber said, hailing the title as "more than just a game" and pointing to the franchise's potential to expand into film and television.

But that launch is also spawning a second economy around the enormous global hype and demand for GTA VI content.

Viewers watched 125.5 million hours of the title's content in its first 24 hours across platforms including Twitch, YouTube and Kick, according to Streams Charts.

YouTube creator Darren "IShowSpeed" Watkins Jr. led streamers with 4.47 million hours viewed across YouTube and Twitch. Separately, Twitch tracker CastAccord recorded about two million hours watched over roughly 36 hours of streamer Kai Cenat's launch broadcast.

Both creators had built anticipation with trailers for their own GTA VI coverage. As of Nov. 19, IShowSpeed's trailer had drawn 3.7 million YouTube views, while Cenat's had amassed 2.7 million.

According to Forbes, IShowSpeed and Cenat are estimated to earn about $30 million and $8.5 million a year, respectively, from their broader creator businesses.

Rockstar Games, the studio behind the franchise, has also moved to capitalize on the surge in viewer interest, partnering with YouTube on a 24-hour launch broadcast and introducing themed gifts that viewers could send during select streams.

Outside creators and agencies are also trying to capture the surge in demand for Grand Theft Auto content.

Agency Clipped & Shipped is offering a $10,000 prize pool to editors who can drive audiences to clips from Kai Cenat's streams, with qualifying submissions distributed across YouTube Shorts, TikTok, Instagram and X.

A separate campaign offered $1 per 1,000 views for clips of streamer KreekCraft, with individual submissions eligible to earn up to $400.

Those audiences could translate into thousands of dollars in creator revenue.

Using AIR Media-Tech's U.S. YouTube Shorts benchmark, 1.5 million eligible engaged views could generate about $490, while 5.1 million would yield roughly $1,670.

The two Vice City Dispatch videos could generate about $3,840 based on higher benchmark rates for long-form content. The channels' actual earnings could not be independently verified.

Moreover, the content economy is becoming increasingly automated.

On X, users are pitching artificial intelligence-assisted clipping operations that scan livestreams for spikes in chat activity, automatically cut highlights and distribute them across multiple accounts.

One user claimed an operation generated $21,000 overnight from 2.7 million views, equivalent to about $7.78 per 1,000 reported views.

The earnings claims could not be independently verified. X bases payouts on qualified impressions from Premium users, which are not reflected in public view counts.

And appetite for more content — and more revenue — may only be starting.

Wedbush analyst Alicia Reese told CNBC that in-game purchases could generate "years of high-margin recurring revenue" for Take-Two, which she believes investors have yet to fully price in.

She expects the console launch to be followed by a PC release and then an online multiplayer mode, mirroring the rollout of the previous title in the franchise.

Rockstar's launch announcement listed PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X and S, with no timetable for either potential follow-on. GTA VI has also picked up eight nominations for the Game Awards, including game of the year, best game direction, narrative, art direction, score and music, audio design, action/adventure and performance.

#2825. Halaifonua scores 22, Natt 21 and Oklahoma State goes wild in second half to rout Virginia Tech

CHARLESTON, S.C. (AP) — Julius Halaifonua scored 22 points, Kashie Natt added 21 and Oklahoma State cut loose for 65 points in the second half to cruise past Virginia Tech 106-80 at the Charleston Classic on Sunday.

The Cowboys had runs of 11-0 and 13-0 in a seven-minute stretch of the second half, turning a close game into a rout. They led 77-59 with 9 1/2 minutes remaining. They added another 29 points in the final eight minutes.

Through a 13-minute stretch of the second half, the Hokies missed 13 of 15 shots and Oklahoma State's lead reached 96-68 when Halaifonua drained a 3-pointer with about 3 minutes remaining.

The Cowboys buried 74% of their shots in the second half, 67% for the game. Virginia Tech shot 39% in the second half, 44% for the game.

Off the bench, Luka Bogavac scored 17 points and Benjamin Ahmed added 11 for the Cowboys (4-2).

Ben Hammond led Virginia Tech (6-1) with 24 points and Jaylen Curry, a transfer from Oklahoma State, added 18.

There were 10 lead changes in the first half and each team had a largest lead of five points. Oklahoma State shot 58% in the half but Virginia Tech kept it close with eight made 3-pointers. The score was 41-41 at the break.

Ben Hammond and Amani Hansberry combined to score Virginia Tech's first 13 points of the second half and the Hokies had their last lead at 54-51 with 16 1/2 minutes left in the game. It was then that Oklahoma State took control.

Virginia Tech had won all five meetings with OSU, though four were decided by six points or fewer.

Oklahoma State: The Cowboys host Jackson State on Wednesday.

Virginia Tech: Old Dominion visits Blacksburg on Friday.

#2824. DK Metcalf ejected after another dustup with Eagles' Cooper DeJean, unsportsmanlike conduct penalty amid Steelers loss

Pittsburgh Steelers wide receiver DK Metcalf had more costly penalties on Sunday at Lincoln Financial Field than receptions.

Metcalf was ejected with only one reception to his name after the second of those infractions late in the third quarter during a 19-16 loss to the Philadelphia Eagles. The final straw was when he was flagged for unsportsmanlike conduct.

He was called for taunting in the wake of his latest dustup with Eagles defensive back Cooper DeJean. And so Metcalf, who was notably suspended two games last season for swinging at a Detroit Lions fan during a sideline altercation, was disqualified, with Philly fans waving goodbye to him as he walked to the tunnel.

The taunting penalty occurred in the fallout of a third-and-8 incompletion from Aaron Rodgers with less than five minutes left in the third quarter. Rodgers was looking for wide receiver Germie Bernard toward the left pylon. Metcalf was lined up on the right side of the field pre-snap, in the slot, where he was matched up with DeJean.

Following the play, the CBS Sports broadcast showed Metcalf and Philadelphia safety Marcus Epps getting into it. Metcalf had to be restrained by Pittsburgh wide receiver Roman Wilson and tight end Pat Freiermuth.

After Metcalf walked off to the sideline, however, the broadcast displayed another clip that exhibited Metcalf putting his hand into DeJean's face mask. DeJean appeared to indicate that he was poked in the eye.

Steelers head coach Mike McCarthy confirmed that accusation postgame, explaining that the referee told him that Metcalf "poked the defender in the eye."

Not only was Metcalf DQ'd, but he also lost his team 15 yards. That backed up a Chris Boswell field-goal attempt, which ended up being from 52 yards out. Fortunately for Pittsburgh, Boswell still made the kick to cut the team's deficit to 16-9.

But, even with some help from little-known wideout Max Hurleman in Metcalf's absence — the former undrafted free agent hauled in a late touchdown after being elevated from the practice squad — the Steelers (6-4) fell by three points.

In turn, the injury-ravaged Eagles (3-7) snapped their seven-game losing streak.

Afterward, McCarthy was asked about his confidence level in Metcalf.

"I think our men do a good job of looking in the mirror, and we got a short week," McCarthy said, alluding to Pittsburgh's Black Friday game against the Denver Broncos coming up.

"But yeah, no, we'll sit down and address everything, especially, you know, we need him. We need him in those games, and that's not acceptable to be thrown out of a game. He's one of the 48 [active players]. It's about everything we do through the week is to put those guys in position to be successful."

Metcalf's first penalty arrived with just under 13 minutes to go in the third quarter. He was blocking DeJean on a 5-yard carry from Pittsburgh running back Jaylen Warren. That turned into extracurricular activity.

Metcalf and DeJean were tangled up after the play. They had to be separated, and Metcalf was assessed an unnecessary roughness penalty.

That infraction effectively erased a positive first-down gain by Warren, keeping the Steelers at the doorstep of their own end zone.

Metcalf clocked out with one catch for two receiving yards in the defeat. He came into the day with 35 receptions, 432 receiving yards and two touchdowns. In the past four games, he's reeled in a combined six passes.

In March 2025, the Steelers acquired Metcalf from the Seattle Seahawks in a trade. Pittsburgh gave up a 2025 second-round pick and signed the two-time Pro Bowl receiver to a five-year extension reportedly worth $150 million.

The Steelers went on to trade wideout George Pickens to the Dallas Cowboys later that offseason.

Metcalf, a 2019 second-round pick, has been ejected three times since entering the NFL and is tied with San Francisco 49ers linebacker Dre Greenlaw for the most disqualifications in that span, according to ESPN. Infamously, Metcalf's aforementioned sideline altercation with a Lions fan last season didn't result in a penalty or an ejection, as it wasn't seen by officials in real time.

#2823. Tim Cowlishaw's Cowboys-Titans report card: A smooth ride into Thanksgiving rivalry game

The Cowboys are this close to owning the AFC South. Check that, Jacksonville remains on the schedule in December so perhaps a "not so fast" is in order. Still, Sunday's 51-30 destruction of the Tennessee Titans gave Dallas a 3-0 record against the South, a 5-6 record overall and by far its most one-sided victory to date. A look at the highlights:

There's only one way to get better than scoring seven touchdowns and a field goal on your eight possessions, and that would be, of course, eight touchdowns. Had the Cowboys needed that eighth one, I have little doubt they would have found a way. Dak Prescott threw for more than 300 yards with just five incompletions, and they gained 155 yards on the ground including two more touchdowns. At 7.6 yards per play with zero punts and zero turnovers, they were masterful against — admittedly — a really poor Titans defense.

Giving up 30 points to Tennessee (the final touchdown came with 31 seconds to play) is not bad at all. The Titans had averaged 27.7 points in their last three losses to winning teams (Jacksonville, Cincinnati, Cleveland). Markquese Bell had a big fumble return on a takeaway created by Quinnen Williams and the Cowboys sacked Cam Ward five times. Williams had a pair of them. Hats off to linebacker Justin Barron with nine tackles.

Dallas Cowboys quarterback Dak Prescott celebrates as he leaves the field following the Cowboys victory over the Tennessee Titans in an NFL football game at AT&T Stadium on Sunday, Nov. 22, 2026, in Arlington. (Smiley N. Pool/The Dallas Morning News)

Probably should go with the incomplete grade when Aubrey goes 1-for-1 on field goals and Bryan Anger has no punts. It's really more of an A for your team when special teams play this tiny of a role (especially on punts). On eight returned kicks, the Cowboys kept the Titans from having a 30-yarder.

This was a no doubter almost from the start, and just because the Titans are bad, an easy afternoon was not a given, considering recent performances against Arizona and Indy. Coach Brian Schottenheimer had the offense in tune all day, and Mike Pettine and Christian Parker, both walking the sideline, seemed to find the right touch with a downtrodden defense.

I think Tennessee would have to be held below 30 to go any higher with this grade. But the Cowboys got the smooth ride into the short week of Thanksgiving that they were seeking and seemed to steer clear of major injuries before getting ready for a tougher game with the Eagles.

#2821. S&P 500 correction: Five forces behind the market sell-off

Esteemed Wharton professor Jeremy Siegel was more animated than usual on Friday afternoon... with good reason.

The normally staid author of "Stocks for the Long Run" spoke to CNBC on Friday as the market careened toward some ugly milestones. The S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average closed in correction territory, off more than 10% from their records. The Nasdaq Composite lost its gain for the year and the more concentrated Nasdaq-100 entered bear market territory on an intraday basis.

"These things are like straws that break the camel's back. You know what I mean? Everyone [is] all, 'I don't care, I don't care cause everything else is good' and when those straws keep on going... Bang," said Siegel.

Just a month-and-a-half ago, the S&P 500 and the Nasdaq Composite were at record highs. But the straws were already adding up by then and a market in denial is just now recognizing them.

Here are the reasons behind the sell-off:

The breaking point came this week in the form of the initial public offering of artificial intelligence leader Anthropic and a pick-up in the selling intensity for one-time AI chip leader Nvidia.

Anthropic upped its offering amount a day before going public despite the already dimming climate around AI-related stocks. The move ensured it would be the biggest IPO ever, but played into the fears that this debut — following SpaceX's monster offering over the summer — was a collective cash-out moment for the AI complex that will leave the investing masses holding the bag.

In other words, it could be the ultimate "sell the news" moment for the AI trade.

On Thursday, Anthropic traded from its $83 IPO price to a high above $115 before instantly rolling over and taking the AI trade with it. Anthropic's sell-off intensified Friday, and when the dust settled Anthropic closed the week at $88.91.

The Nasdaq posted its worst week since the Covid plunge of March 2020, down 10%.

Nvidia fell 14% on Friday with the selling intensifying after a Reuters headline hit the tape that the chipmaker's accounting is the focus of a Securities and Exchange Commission probe.

Nvidia is now down nearly 40% in November alone, a month that saw the AI bull market's leading salesman, Jensen Huang, resign as its CEO because of an affair with an alleged Chinese spy that raised speculation about whether the company's chips were ending up in Beijing's hands despite government restrictions.

To start the week, the Commerce Department tightened restrictions on U.S.-made chips to stop China from accessing them remotely from other countries.

A solid earnings report on Tuesday fell flat as Nvidia's board definitively ruled out any chance Huang would return, disappointing some of the stock's fanatics still holding out hope for his far-fetched return. Nvidia ended the week with a 23% loss.

The S&P 500 traded below its 200-day moving average — what's considered a measure of a security's long-term trend — on Tuesday and made a convincing break of it on Thursday. This turned many on Wall Street— like commodity trading advisors — into automatic sellers on the market. So-called CTAs are individuals or organizations that advise others on trading futures and swaps across a range of assets.

"We know there's a lot of trend riders on these markets," said Siegel. "They have their trend lines. They've ridden it up and as soon as it goes down, they're selling and they're selling and they're selling and they just perpetuate" the decline, he said.

The Iran conflict has caused the price oil to surge and while it's off its highs it still remains above $83 a barrel, while the fees for transiting the Strait of Hormuz and the rest of the region keep persistent pressure on global prices.

A series of numbers earlier in the month showed inflation is still far from the Federal Reserve's 2% target, sparking a run of central bank officials to warn they may have to get more aggressive with interest rates.

After increasing rates in September by a quarter point, the Fed skipped a hike in late October and sparked concern among bond investors it may be falling behind the inflation fight. The 10-year Treasury yield subsequently jumped above 5% and stayed there until a flight of safe haven fixed income buying brought it back below that level on Friday.

Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller said this week that the Fed may need to do a half percentage point hike next month to catch up.

"I'm very, very concerned about inflation. It's kind of headed in the wrong direction, and it's spreading out," Waller told CNBC.

"Don't fight the Fed" has long been a maxim on Wall Street and right now expectations are that the central bank will need to raise rates in December and again in January.

And late-breaking on Friday was a report from the Financial Times that the Trump Administration is considering restarting strikes on Iran.

Before the grim market milestones were met on Friday, it had long lost its mojo on fears that the pace of growth driven by AI companies was slowing.

As Anthropic prepared to go public, its third-quarter figures released earlier this month showed revenue growth last quarter was 47%, down from 144% in the second quarter.

AI momentum trades, like the VanEck Semiconductor ETF and the iShares MSCI USA Momentum Factor ETF, all topped out during the summer.

The fears of slowing growth picked up this month after the Democrats won control of Congress and key governorships, fueling speculation that states — or even the whole country — could implement stricter rules on data center construction.

CoreWeave, which builds data centers using high amounts of debt, is down 71% from its high. Caterpillar, which has reinvented itself as an AI play because of data center construction and generators, is down more than 50% from its high.

The view of Siegel and many market observers is that the economy remains in good enough shape that this will just be a run-of-the-mill correction that resets a bull market that had raised people's expectations too high because of its early ferocity.

The S&P 500 returned more than 20% in 2023 and 2024 and then gained 16% last year.

"The average over long periods for the stock market is 8%, 9% a year up," said Siegel. "People began to think that 15 to 20 [percent] was going to be the norm with AI and we have to bring that down to reality."

The only problem with that theory is that the stock market and its wealth effect was one of the major factors underpinning the economy. Stocks are a greater source of household wealth — especially for baby boomers — than they ever have been before.

With the S&P 500 clinging to a 1% gain for the year, this stock market rout could end up tipping off a virtuous cycle that slows the economy, and in turn, leads to bigger equity losses.

#2820. Wembanyama hits 50 points in dominant Spurs win

San Antonio Spurs star Victor Wembanyama hit 50 points in a historic performance as his side beat the New Orleans Pelicans 114-91.

The 22-year-old, who is 7ft 4ins tall, became the first player with 50 points, five blocks and five three-pointers in a match since the NBA added threes in 1979–80.

The France international, who was defensive player of the year for 2026, said he put on a show for a celebrity-packed crowd which included actress and long-time Spurs fan Selena Gomez.

"I just want to keep stacking them [records] up. I don't plan on doing those. They just come and we play the right way as a team.

"So, I just want to stack them up. Hopefully there's many more coming."

The Spurs have made a fine start to the season, winning 14 of their 17 games so far to top the Western Conference Southwest.

They won the division last season before losing to the New York Knicks in the NBA Finals.

Spurs coach Mitch Johnson added. "It's a cliche to say, 'Why doesn't he do it every game?'

"But it's so interesting when people play like that, especially with his talent."

Wembanyama's haul - the third of his career - came after James Harden scored 51 points to move third on the all-time list for 50-point NBA games on Saturday.

Wilt Chamberlain holds the record with an incredible 118 50-point games.

"It is very special. But unlike many of my peers, I'm not very good at stat-padding," Wembanyama said.

"So I haven't reached it as many times as I should have."

Wembanyama was later asked whether there was an ethical right or wrong way to score 50 points. Some NBA players have been accused of chasing individual statistics for financial gain instead of concentrating on their team's results.

"Maybe, I think that's a question for you. The greats, they don't think about that," Wembanyama replied.

"I don't think LeBron [James], Shai [Gilgeous-Alexander], [Nikola] Jokic, [Luka] Doncic, I don't think they think about that. I have them as examples, so I'm trying to not think about it either."

#2819. Mets reportedly sign Tarik Skubal for nine years, $450 million: Breaking down the record-setting deal

For the second offseason in the last three years, the New York Mets have landed the biggest player on the market, reportedly inking Tarik Skubal to a record-setting, nine-year, $450 million contract, according to ESPN's Jeff Passan.

The contract sets a new record for total guaranteed money and average annual value (AAV) for a pitching-only contract. That has previously been set by Yoshinobu Yamamoto's 12-year, $325 million contract with the Dodgers. There are no opt-outs in the contract, and Skubal has a full no-trade clause, which means there's a strong likelihood that the 30-year-old left-hander will end his career with the Mets.

MLB Free Agent Tracker 2026-27: Top players available, latest signings, and contract information

Stay up to date with the MLB free agent market this offseason, including player signings, contract details, and more as the 2026-27 Hot Stove gets underway.

As The Athletic's Jayson Stark noted, "The Mets now employ the highest-paid position player (Juan Soto) and pitcher (Tarik Shubal). They are on the hook for $907.3 million, just for those two players, for the next nine seasons." That's an interesting juxtaposition with the ongoing dispute between the owners and the MLB Players Association about imposing a salary cap, and Mets owner Steve Cohen making this deal doesn't seem like a person who is worried about having to adhere to a cap in the near future.

With Skubal now off the board, the free agent market has now been set for other starting pitchers like Freddy Peralta, Michael King, Trevor Rogers, Kevin Gausman, and others. But what does this mean for the Mets' rotation?

Obviously, Skubal will be positioned as the ace for a franchise that desperately needed one. In 2026, the Mets' starters were ninth in strikeout rate (23.3%), 22nd in WHIP (1.33), 23rd in ERA (4.47), 24th in walk rate (9.1%), and just 26th in wins (38). They needed an anchor, and they got one.

Skubal pitched to a 2.72 ERA, 0.96 WHIP, and 186/27 K/BB ratio in 158 2/3 innings despite having to miss six weeks following elbow surgery. That procedure, which was done with NanoScope technology, allowed Skubal to return faster than is typical when a pitcher needs to have loose bodies removed from their elbows. It also didn't seem to have any impact on his performance, as Skubal pitched to a 2.36 ERA over his final 16 starts. In fact, in 10 starts with the Dodgers after the trade deadline, Skubal had a 2.61 ERA, 1.05 WHIP, and a 70/13 K/BB ratio in 62 innings.

There are some long-term health concerns with Skubal, which need to be taken into account given the length of this deal. He had Tommy John surgery back in 2016 before his professional career began. He then had flexor tendon surgery in September of 2022 and then had this elbow procedure in May of this past season. The left-hander has pitched to a 2.47 ERA since returning from that flexor tendon surgery in 2023, so the previous injuries have not impacted his performance, but you do have to wonder how many more years the Mets will get at peak production.

With Tarik Skubal in New York, the Mets can now slide Nolan McLean down to the number two spot in their rotation. Christian Scott seems locked into another spot after his strong 2026 season, but Zac Thornton, Sean Manaea, Kodai Senga, Tylor Megill, Jonah Tong are all options for the final two spots. Which also means we should not rule out the Mets adding another starting pitcher in free agency.

McLean is a talented young pitcher and had a good season with a 3.16 ERA, 1.17 WHIP, and 194/70 K/BB ratio. The 25-year-old is a future ace, but it's hard to rely on him as the ace of a potential World Series contender in 2027. Last year was his first full MLB season and his only season in professional baseball with over 113 innings pitched. McLean has also struggled to find a secondary pitch he can consistently rely on for whiffs against righties, and he posted a below-average 9.9% swinging strike rate against right-handed hitters in 2026. The addition of Skubal gives him continued time to develop without having to carry a rotation.

Scott is another arm that will benefit from a veteran like Skubal. Scott pitched 110 innings in 2026 after missing all of 2025 while recovering from Tommy John surgery. The 27-year-old registered a 3.85 ERA, 1.27 WHIP, and 134/47 K/BB. He posted a solid 12.1% swinging strike rate and has continued to tweak his arsenal, adding in a cutter to help him handle left-handed hitters.

Skubal, who has an elite changeup, could also be a big help to Thornton, who had a 3.42 ERA and 1.14 WHIP in 15 starts for the Mets in 2026 but lacks a changeup that can be a real weapon against right-handed batters. With Skubal in the mix, the Mets could also feel better about keeping Senga in the bullpen, where he posted a 4.85 ERA but a 4.16 xFIP and a nearly 28% strikeout rate.

All in all, the move clearly positions the Mets to be one of the favorites in the National League in 2027, but don't assume that it also means they're done adding to their rotation this offseason.

#2817. Trump says there could be a 'positive spin' from the latest FBI database hack

President Donald Trump suggested Saturday that there could be a bright spot in the latest incursion into the FBI computer database.

The president confirmed in an interview with Fox News that a system has been breached that houses information used by law enforcement at all levels to share criminal justice information.

The hackers accessed the National Criminal Information Center (NCIC), a database that includes FBI fingerprint records, wanted persons, violent felons, missing persons and related information, two people familiar with the matter told MS NOW. The database is restricted to law enforcement use but not classified and it is unclear what data was extracted.

CNN had first reported on the suspected hack, citing sources familiar with the issue.

In a wide-ranging interview on Fox, Trump said there could be upside from the news.

"Maybe we'll learn about the voter fraud, all the voter fraud that took place," he said. "I always like to put things on a positive spin."

This would be the second recent intrusion into the FBI system; the previous one came in September, when a group known as "ShinyHunters" broke into an FBI jobs website. The result compromised data on current and former FBI employees and shut down part of the agency's operations until the issue was resolved.

Trump said it's possible there could be information gleaned from the latest hack.

"I wonder who's doing it, because wouldn't it be great to learn about Russia, Russia, Russia?" he said. "People should hear about it, because it actually put our country in danger, and so maybe we'll learn things that we wouldn't have learned otherwise."

The FBI did not immediately respond to a CNBC request for comment.

Barlow looks set for season debut with Sixers after long days, 'miserable' weeks originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Dominick Barlow is on the verge of his season debut with the Sixers.

The team listed Barlow as probable for its Saturday afternoon matchup with the Bucks at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

He was a reliable rotation fixture last season, playing 71 regular-season games and starting 59. Barlow posted 7.7 points, 4.8 rebounds, 1.2 assists and 0.9 steals per contest in 23.8 minutes per game. The Sixers then made a common-sense call to exercise his $3.4 million option for the 2026-27 season.

The 23-year-old described his back injury as "an overuse thing."

"I guess there was an area or maybe I started my (offseason) workouts too early," he said. "I don't know what it was fully in terms of what caused it. Once it happened, it happened. Nothing I can do about it besides try to correct it and get my body feeling as good as possible."

He called the past few weeks "miserable" as he rehabbed and waited on the green light by the Sixers' training staff.

"The days just felt longer," Barlow said. "Trying to be engaged mentally, which I think I've done a good job with, but the physical aspect wasn't there. I'm somebody that's prided himself on competing, being available. Not being able to do that was hard, but I'm excited to kind of put this past me."

With LeBron James and Jaylen Brown on the team now, Barlow won't start at forward when the Sixers are at full strength.

"Just cutting, playing hard, being ready to make the open shot," Barlow said. "And then I don't think you can ever have too much defensive versatility. I think that's something I can bring, try to help us be more versatile."

When Barlow checks into his first game of the season, he'll appreciate the moment.

"You don't really realize what you have until it's not there (anymore)," he said. "I'm just excited to play. …. Just playing the first stint … that's going to be the main thing I'm looking forward to."

Dean Wade (left hamstring soreness/tightness)

#2813. Aaron Rodgers, DK Metcalf can't fix Steelers' broken passing game after Eagles loss

The Pittsburgh Steelers had another ugly day offensively and dropped a 19-9 decision to the Philadelphia Eagles on Nov. 22 at Lincoln Financial Field.

Top receiver DK Metcalf was ejected after multiple personal-foul penalties as the passing game struggled. Quarterback Aaron Rodgers finished the day with 30 completions on 40 attempts for 227 yards and a touchdown.

On the other side, the defense played fairly well, but not well enough to cope with the lack of scoring from the offense.

Here's USA TODAY Sports' report card on the action from a Pittsburgh perspective.

Required reading: Kevin Harlan has blunt assessment of Steelers WR DK Metcalf's ejection

Rodgers simply isn't a threat to beat defenses downfield consistently right now. He was able to get some momentum going at times with the short and quick passes that have proven effective for the Steelers at a few different points this season. It's also true that his receivers need to be open consistently for him to hit some of the more explosive plays. They frequently are not. None of that changes the reality that the lack of a deeper passing game is a major problem for this offense right now, and a near 43-year-old quarterback needs to share responsibility for the failures with everyone else, whether his career is likely to end at the Pro Football Hall of Fame or not.

For the second game in a row, Jaylen Warren wasn't able to make practically anyone miss. The blocking wasn't great, but Warren frequently hasn't needed it to be to transcend and make a lot of explosive plays this season. With left guard Mason McCormick out, possibly for a while, the sledding isn't likely to get easier. The Steelers need more elusiveness from him and primary backup Rico Dowdle. Warren had 28 yards on nine carries. Dowdle had 15 on five touches.

Roman Wilson's return to the lineup didn't lead to much meaningful direct production, nor did it seem to make life easier for top receivers DK Metcalf and Germie Bernard. The former was frequently invisible, as he was in the first half of the loss to Cincinnati last week, and he got himself ejected with a pair of senseless personal-foul penalties. His numbers continue to track toward career lows and the conduct is only compounding the headache he's becoming. Bernard made some of those plays in the short and quick range, which was an encouraging sign after he was held off the scoresheet by the Bengals. Practice squad veteran Max Hurleman added his first NFL touchdown late. It wasn't a performance that engendered much confidence in the group as a whole, though.

Pat Freiermuth was more active than he has been recently as a safety valve for Rodgers, but the downfield success still was not there. Darnell Washington was also relatively quiet. As the receivers continue to struggle, you'd like to see more from these two. They're both playing on lengthy contract extensions to make more plays to jump-start this struggling offense.

There was no push ahead of Dowdle and Warren in the running game. In the passing game, Rodgers was scrambling to avoid pressure frequently and was sacked five times. Much of the momentum this group seemed to establish before the bye has dissipated. The loss of McCormick is certainly disruptive, so there's a temptation to give these guys some benefit of the doubt as they figure life out without him. The results need to be better even with that caveat, though, particularly in the running game. This offense will quickly become one dimensional if this group can't figure it out.

Saquon Barkley's 38-yard touchdown run was a game changer that this group was primarily responsible for. Cam Heyward and Co. did well to contain him otherwise - he had just 37 yards beyond that one big play - and were frequently in the backfield making plays. With the offense playing the way it was, the team just needed them to be better against a struggling offense like Philadelphia's.

Patrick Queen was all over the ball yet again. He's playing some of the best football of his life within DC Patrick Graham's defense right now. Alex Highsmith forced a fumble and generated two long-awaited sacks before an injury disrupted his day. T.J. Watt was once again mostly invisible, except while he was beaten badly on the back side of the long Barkley touchdown run.

Eagles quarterback Joe Milton had chances to make the Steelers pay but missed with some poorly timed overthrows into solid coverage. Beyond a 38-yard completion to Dallas Goedert, the Pittsburgh defensive backs avoided most of the explosive plays that cost them dearly against the Bengals. That was a positive step forward for this soft zone scheme.

McCarthy does not seem to have control over whatever is going on with Metcalf this season. For as much criticism as Mike Tomlin took for being too much of a "player's coach," it's fair to question what's going on in McCarthy's locker room when his top receiver not only can't produce, but also can't stay on the field because of his temper. The lack of downfield passing is also a reflection of his playcalling.

#2812. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2811. Quantum computing’s first real-world payoff may come in energy, not drug discovery

For years, the case for quantum computing has rested on ambitious promises, from discovering new drugs by simulating how molecules behave to optimizing supply chains and airline schedules. Many of the biggest aims are still likely to be years, if not decades, away.

But quantum software, which uses algorithms designed to solve complex problems faster than conventional computers, is already being tested on a more immediate challenge: the energy transition, from keeping power grids stable to finding better materials for solar cells and hydrogen production.

Phasecraft, a quantum software company founded in Bristol, England, in 2019, is one of the firms putting the technology to work in energy. It raised $34 million in Series B funding in 2025, and in September this year announced plans to set up U.S. headquarters in Arlington, Virginia.

There are enormous opportunities to solve problems that are both important for energy security and commercially valuable, Ashley Montanaro, co-founder and CEO of Phasecraft, told CNBC.

Phasecraft isn't alone in applying quantum software to the energy industry.

Quantinuum, which was formed by the merger of Cambridge Quantum and Honeywell Quantum Solutions and listed on Nasdaq in June, is using the technology to model materials for hydrogen fuel cells for transportation. It is also working with partners on materials for carbon capture.

Another U.K. player, ORCA Computing, a spinout from the University of Oxford, has partnered with BP to better understand how a molecule's physical, chemical and biological properties are governed. That could help explain how biofuels burn and how to make them burn more efficiently

In a separate project, ORCA worked with Japanese software company JIJ on a challenge power companies face: deciding which generators to switch on or off, and when, to meet demand at the lowest cost.

That project also involved BP and the National Quantum Computing Centre (NQCC), the U.K.'s national laboratory for quantum computing, which is part of the government-backed U.K. Research and Innovation. The involvement of the NQCC, like that of ARPA-E, suggests governments are paying close attention to quantum software's potential role in energy, as are companies such as BP.

For Phasecraft, a priority is grid stability and optimization. It has partnered with the National Energy System Operator (NESO), the independent system planner that balances energy supply and demand in England, Scotland and Wales, on grid optimization tasks, including managing power exchanges and isolating network sections during emergencies. The company says its software can complete these tasks up to 1,000 times faster than conventional computing.

Phasecraft believes its grid software could be particularly sought after in Asia, where electrification is rapidly expanding, increasing the need for grid resilience to balance high and growing demand with the intermittent supply offered by renewables.

The company is also working with U.K. solar cell maker Oxford PV on the development of new materials for photovoltaic cells capable of operating more efficiently than existing ones.

Separately, it has teamed up with London-listed specialty materials developer Johnson Matthey, Harvard University and Boston-based quantum hardware specialist QuEra, in work overseen by the U.S. government's Advanced Research Projects Agency–Energy (ARPA-E), to discover novel catalysts that would reduce reliance on scarce metals like iridium — which is currently critical in the production of hydrogen.

Asked why Phasecraft is focusing on energy rather than sectors that might seem more lucrative, Montanaro said the returns were there too.

"When you get this right, the commercial returns are real: more efficient energy networks mean lower operating costs and more value from existing infrastructure," he said.

#2810. Atalanta grab dramatic draw with Inter in Serie A

Atalanta rescued an extraordinary late 4-4 draw against Inter Milan on Sunday as the champions fell three points off the Serie A title pace despite Francesco Pio Esposito's hat-trick, while AC Milan and Juventus claimed big wins.

Inter looked comfortable in Bergamo when Petar Sucic's penalty gave them a two-goal cushion in the 76th minute after Esposito's treble, but Charles De Ketelaere's second goal of the night and a stoppage-time Franck Kessie penalty salvaged a point for La Dea.

It marked only the third time Inter have dropped points in the league this season but kept Cristian Chivu's side in third place, one point behind Roma and three adrift of leaders Lazio.

AC Milan stayed in the title mix by going fourth, two points behind Inter, with a 5-1 win over Frosinone, while Juventus moved into the top six by thrashing Venezia 4-0 in Turin.

Inter's result continued a theme of defensive frailty, having conceded 17 goals in 12 league games this season.

"We managed to always have a two-goal lead, then we failed to show maturity in the final moments," Chivu told DAZN.

"It's hard to explain, we weren't able to manage and maintain the lead we created. We have thrown away two points, but we will learn the lesson and move on."

Esposito struck twice in the first half, pouncing on a deflected ball in the box to sweep home the opener before doubling Inter's lead by heading home from a corner.

Eljif Elmas quickly got one back for the hosts to keep the game alive, but Esposito took his third in composed fashion early in the second half, marking his eighth goal in five games for club and country.

De Ketelaere proved the catalyst for Atalanta's fightback, pulling a goal back with a header and firing home a powerful finish either side of Sucic's spot-kick.

That set up a thrilling conclusion as Atalanta chased what had seemed an unlikely point, which they claimed when a penalty was awarded for handball that Kessie calmly converted.

Milan returned to the top four as Goncalo Ramos ended his goal drought in a 5-1 thrashing of Frosinone to seal back-to-back wins for the first time in over a month.

Ruben Amorim's side got off to a flying start with a Gabriele Calvani own goal and a composed Ramos strike inside 19 minutes.

Amorim accused his players of "not helping Ramos" ahead of the game after the Portugal international scored just twice in his first 11 league games following a 74-million-euro ($86 million) summer move from Paris Saint-Germain.

His third goal of the season was met with a loud roar from the San Siro crowd after he was teed up by France international Adrien Rabiot, who became the first Milan midfielder since Kaka in 2004 to notch a goal or assist in seven consecutive league games.

The visitors soon hit back through Patrizio Masini, but Milan pulled clear in the second half with some lethal counter-attacking as Ardon Jashari, Alphadjo Cisse and Christian Pulisic struck to complete the biggest victory of the Amorim era.

"Frosinone are all about full pressure. I understood that coming to San Siro with a lot of points, they would be really brave," Amorim told DAZN.

"We changed a little bit the way we play the game because of the opponent, and it was a good night for us."

Juventus moved up to sixth thanks to a second-half surge in a 4-0 win over Venezia in Turin.

Coach Luciano Spalletti faced scrutiny after an underwhelming start to the season, with Antonio Conte linked with a return to his former club during the international break.

But Spalletti celebrated becoming just the sixth coach to reach 600 Serie A games with a resounding win, despite his team being booed off at the break following a goalless first half.

Nick Woltemade came off the bench at half-time to score one goal and set up another for Teun Koopmeiners, before a Juan Cabal header and Randal Kolo Muani penalty wrapped up an 18-minute blitz.

Milutin Osmajic scored a double in a 2-0 win for Genoa at Sassuolo and Bologna were held to a 1-1 draw by Udinese.
